## Nivian

Nivian was a city in the ancient Illefarn that existed in the -13 century DR.

Culture
The most notable tradition of the ancient Nivian was the annual non-lethal grand melee championship between champions of the city's six districts. The reward for the winner was fame, while the winning district gained possession of a certain stature that depicted a local hero. In order to avoid chaos, each district selected its champion from a set of willing individuals who eagerly received the patronage of the district's noble families.

History
In the, Nivian's Artisan's District selected its champion – Beloran, who showed impressive commitment and drive to become the championship's winner. Beloran reprinted his district six years in a row, never claiming the grand title. The man was madly in love with the city ruler's niece – Ilum Lura, and Beloran's only goal was to gain her favor. However, Ilum showed little interest in the Artisan's District's champion, always politely turning him down. Ilum's father exercised his civic pride by ordering the beautiful woman to give Beloran a token of her favor, a set of enchanted riding gloves, many centuries later known as the Favor of Ilum Lura. Beloran accepted her reluctant gift and pinned it to his sword arm shoulder, as the tradition dictated.

Inspired by the token, the champion fought fiercer than ever and claimed the grand title of Nivian's champion for the first time. Now Ilum Lura, who offered her token of favor, owed the champion a kiss, another traditional reward for the winner. Beloran received his kiss, but Ilum left the love-stricken champion immediately after. For several months after his win, Beloran continued his fruitless attempts to kindle a relationship with the beautiful noblewoman. Exhausted of his advances, Ilum agreed to meet with Beloran, only to inform him that her heart belonged to another.

Heartbroken and driven mad by his unrequited love, Beloran left Nivian, never to e seen again. Those who witnessed the champion's departure said that he still wore the Favor of Ilum Lura pinned on his sword arm shoulder.

As many wizards in his school of magic, Niri Eddin gained numerous secret enemies and he planned to deal with them in a gruesome manner. ## Night Plague

The Night Plague was a critical event in the history of the city of Zhentil Keep in the Moonsea region.

Activities
The original followers of Zhentar grew their powers and gradually faded from public view or passed their positions on to others. What the inhabitants of Zhentil Keep did not know was that Zhentar's followers grouped together in the crypts beneath the city and achieved lichdom. Unlike most other liches, the liches, who were later called "Those Who Walk By Night", worked together to expand Zhentar's original goals. However, the passage of time slowly eroded their minds to the point where they eventually rose up to haunt the streets of Zhentil Keep beginning in the .

Businesses began to close early and people barricaded themselves in inns and taverns. Only the brave or foolish walked the streets at night. It was only after several notable merchants were slain and commerce began to suffer that the Lords decided to take any action. The Zhentilar began to grow as the need for a proper defense mounted. Skilled soldiers, backed by powerful wizards and priests of Bane, swore fealty to the Council of Lords. The wizards in control of the Zhentilar cared little for the threat from the Night Plague, but used it as a convenient excuse to bolster their grip on the city.

They did indeed clash mightily with the liches, but at least a few of those battles were surely staged for dramatic effect. The only outcome from such clashes was the destruction of property of Lords who opposed the Zhentilar. In truth the Zhentilar bribed, coerced, and threatened the liches until they agreed to return underground where they belonged. The only lich truly slain during this time was Vingril, but an adventuring company called the Golden Stag took the credit for that.

The liches still existed as of the mid–14 century DR and were still alive in the Zhents' memory, although probably most of them had been destroyed, incapacitated, or grown insane with the passing of time, if they hadn't attained demilichdom already and moved onto other goals. Zhentish legends tell that Those Who Walk by Night were magically bound into their crypts by Zhentilar spells. People feared they would be released the day the Zhentilar lords no longer ruled the city.

## Night twist

Night twists were deadly, carnivorous trees whose despair-inducing "song" drew victims to them and to death.

Description
Night twists looked more or less like normal (dead) trees, with black bark and no leaves, twisted into unnatural and unsettling shapes. Mature night twists were at least  tall and, while ancient ones could be as much as  tall and . They gave the overall impression of gloomy twilight, nightmares, and sorrow.

Personality
Night twists cared not what suffering they caused, only that they ate.

Abilities
Night twists, as plants, lacked eyes, but were nonetheless reported to possess low-light vision; they did not speak but understood the Common, Elven, and Sylvan languages (though reports of truly ancient night twists claimed that they did speak). They were mildly resistant to most damage except that caused by edged weapons, and were vulnerable to fire. They were possessed of an unholy grace that helped protect them.

Night twists could, at will, unleash a blast of wind akin to a gust of wind spell. Night twists had several other spell-like abilities, being able to replicate blight, deeper darkness, entangle, fear, and phantasmal killer each thrice per day. Ancient night twists could replicate phantasmal killer five times per day, blight, circle of death, deeper darkness, entangle, fear, and insanity all thrice each day, and weird once each day.

Night twists, each evening, made a sorrowful "song" that induced melancholy and despair. This song went as far as at least  and effected victims similarly to the crushing despair spell. Victims so afflicted were driven to seek out the night twist, the source of their despair, to the exclusion of any other activities. If physically restrained, they would slowly wither and die unless the curse was removed. The curse could be removed by limited wish or stronger magic, or by the song of freedom bards could sing. It also ended when the night twist attacked their prey.

The last ability inherent to night twists was that any who slew one was cursed, as if by a nightmare spell. This curse could only be removed by limited wish or stronger magic, and only if cast while the victim was in the throes of the nightmares. If such a victim died while under the curse and was interred, a night twist sapling grew over their grave.

Combat
Night twists attacked primarily with their spell-like abilities, but could also attacked with their branches. It preferred to open with phantasmal killer, and used entangle and deeper darkness to keep prey from escaping. If one felt threatened, it used fear and blasts of wind to drive attackers away.

Ecology
Night twists lived in temperate marshes, where they hid amongst normal plants with some success. Immature night twists seemed as normal, unintelligent plants until they reached maturity. They fed on the rotting corpses of creatures and travelers drawn by its deathly song, which was variously said to sound like a woman weeping or a cold wind blowing over a desolate graveyard; mostly likely, the song was interpreted as sounding like the most sorrowful thing each traveler could imagine.

In Faerûn, night twists were recorded living in the depths of Cormanthor, taking root near fallen temples, the tombs of elf kings, and the bones of slain dragons; they threatened travelers seeking Myth Drannor; and their songs could be heard as far west as the Silver Marches, particularly in the Far Forest near Masulk's Tomb and Taerymdoom.

## Nightbringer

Nightbringers were specialty priests of the Church of Shar who were dedicated to bringing darkness to all and opposing Selûne and her faithful.

Culture
They were true children of Shar who enjoyed invoking paranoia and terror in others by saying phrases like "What could the darkness be concealing?"

Abilities
Nightbringers were skilled in moving silently and concealing themselves in shadows. When facing followers of Selûne in combat they were inspired by Shar and found it easy to strike and harm their opponents. They had a number of spell-like abilities including darkness and continual darkness that could be used several times per day. They could call upon Shar's power once during a week of the full moon, imparting the benefits of a potion of heroism upon them.

They were affected negatively in combat when fighting during day or under the light of a full moon. They were unable to use spells from the sun and weather spheres, and could only use the reverse forms of any spells from the light sphere.

Possessions
A nightbringer typically wore a tunic and leggings of purple cloth trimmed with black, and a large hooded cloak of the same coloration. Shar's holy symbol adorned the front of their tunic. The weapons they carried were easily concealed. Nightbringers could wear any armor they desired, but most preferred armor that suited stealth and flexibility.

Nine Stars Around a Silver Moon was published in the  and a written copy of the ballad was kept in the great fortress-library of Candlekeep as of the . ## Nighthunter (Set)

Nighthunters were specialty priests devoted to Set, the Lord of Evil within the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Activities
Nighthunters participated in countless schemes and plans designed to undermine the authorities and other gods in Mulhorand.

Culture
All nighthunters were humans or half-orcs of Mulhorandi lineage. They kept their heads clean shaven.

Abilities
They were quite stealthy and able to sneak up on targets and stab them in the back like an assassin. They radiated protection from good, 10' radius and were permanently masked by an undetectable lie. They were skilled at crafting deadly poisons and eventually became immune to all poisons. All nighthunters could see well in the dark. Their sinister reputation gathered like-minded individuals to serve them. Nighthunters could cast summon divine minion once per day. Particularly powerful nighthunters could coat any held weapon with lethal poison on touch.

Possessions
In addition to any blunt weapons, a nighthunter could carry a dagger, darts, longbow, blowgun, khopesh, scimitar, spear, or whip. They did not have armor restrictions.

## Nil Sheafrustle

Nil Sheafrustle was one of the engineers of the Lantan-Samarach portal in the jungles of Samarlogh in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Nil was stout and too paunchy for a gnome. He was nervous and fidgety individual who continually checked and tested his equipment to ensure everything was in working order.

History
As of the, Nil Sheafrustle commuted from Lantan to the illegal logging operation in Samarach every morning. His tasks included camp's mechanisms maintenance. Nil belonged to a wealthy gnome family that invested into the operations, so he had personal stake in ensuring the portal stayed operational. In 1374 DR, Nil was authorized to pay bounties for killing nasty pests and more dangerous creatures that roamed the areas around the camp.

## Nilunele Gudelmer

Nilunele Gudelmer was a member of the Gudelmer merchant family and a daughter of Albaerlus Gudelmer who resided in Athkatla in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Like her sister, Nilunele Gudelmer was a smart, astute, and charming woman. Among her family, Nilunele was the most adventurous and eager to partake in bold affairs.

History
By the late 14 century DR, Nilunele Gudelmer had three siblings, all sisters. Iylandra and Raeveshara Gudelmer were the older siblings, while Quaerthara Gudelmer was Nilunele's younger twin. The sisters lived with their father, Albaerlus, in the family mansion, famously haunted and beautiful Dancing Dolphins House in Athkatla's resplendent Gem District. All four sisters were professional escorts and guides to Athkatla, offering their services to visitors in need of companionship. The Gudelmer sisters were well-known in Athkatla's upper classes through their social status and professional life. Nilunele's natural thrill-seeking personality and devil-may-care attitude often made her the first choice to be hired to wear disguises and aid in various illicit activities.

## Nimra

Nimra, nicknamed Nimra Ninehands after one of her spells, was a wizard of Myth Drannor who became imprisoned in the shape of a magical staff until the mid–14 century DR.

Description
She was slim and grey-eyed, but her eyes turned clear blue shortly after her imprisonment ended.

As a staff, she was a length of smooth-polished shadowtop wood.

History
Nimra was imprisoned in the shape of a staff by a rival, Nerndel, around the year, 700 years prior to . Before that time, she had imprisoned herself a number of creatures that her own imprisonment released; these things sought her out unsuccessfully, as her imprisonment made her nigh-impossible to find.

Nimra's imprisonment came as she owed Nerndel six services. Her first service was to train him; her second service was to turn herself into a staff. However, her imprisonment made it impossible for her to complete the first service either. Elminster Aumar also stole from Nerndel the spells that bound her, and made it impossible to release her, so that she would remain safe while her seekers were destroyed.

Nerndel later bequeathed his staff unto Tarth Hornwood, who nearly used up her power (and thus her lifeforce), adventuring across the Dragon Reach and Myth Drannor. When Tarth later sought training from Elminster Aumar in late Mirtul of 1360 DR, the mage requested that his payment be the staff- or rather, its destruction. Nerndel sought out Sarlin to create a duplicate of his staff, so as to destroy that one instead; Elminster saw through the ruse, and Tarth finally destroyed the staff, releasing Nimra.

After her release, Nimra was to complete Tarth Hornwood's training, as he was the heir of Nerndel, whose web of spells still bound her.

## Nightsedge

Nightsedge was a small village located east of the city of Neverwinter on the Sword Coast North.

Geography
The village sat along the northern bank of the Neverwinter River and at the western edge of the Neverwinter Wood. It neighbored the small town of Floodblest on the opposite bank of the river.

Relations
Its place on the border of the Neverwinter Wood meant Nightsedge was often faced with danger.

History
Circa 1357 DR, Nightsedge was beset by frequent orc attacks. These orcs were not from the Trollmoors as were typically roamed this region, but came from somewhere else. Worried and overwhelmed, the Lord Mayor of Nightsedge appealed for aid to Lord Nasher of Neverwinter, who put out a call for adventurers to go to Nightsedge and lend themselves to its defense.

## Ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood

The ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood was an enchanted blade used by members of the Scarlet Brotherhood on the world of Oerth.

Powers
Ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood were deadly weapons, being similar in their enchantments to scimitars of speed. They allowed the wielder to land more slashes in a shorter period of time. Additionally, these blades were enhanced with a potent +3 enchantment to their aim and deadliness. Each slash of such ninjatō introduced deadly poison into the wounds. Lastly, when unsheathed, ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood glowed with a blood-red radiance.

History
These swords originated on Oerth, where they were rewards given to the members of the Scarlet Brotherhood who successfully eliminated the organization's "great enemies." How or when one of these blades arrived on Toril was unknown. However, in the, a ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood, along with several other magic items from Icewind Dale, ended up in the hands of the merchant named Joluv who traveled from Icewind Dale to the City of Coin, Athkatla, to sell the items to wealthy Amnian adventurers. Joluv sold his wares from the Copper Coronet in Athkatla's River District.

## Nine Fires

The Nine Fires  was a fine inn of Arabel that provided good service at low prices.

Location
The inn was on the outermost street of Arabel, just inside of the southeastern city walls, and a block east along the same street from the Wink and Kiss tavern.

Interior
The Nine Fires was a cozy and heavily-decorated place to relax and find rooms. The nine fires for which it was named were a set of hearths placed in the large common room and in the corridors upstairs, and with the tending of the staff kept the inn very warm in winter. A mage was kept on-staff specifically to watch the fires and contain any accidents and disasters in the making, armed with a decanter of endless water, a wand of frost, and a selection of fire-fighting spells.

Services
Though a quiet and under-appreciated spot in Arabel, the Niner offered surprisingly good service at low prices, and guests recieved freshly-made hot meals upstairs via dumbwaiter.

The inn kept a collection of well-used books that guests were encouraged to read during their stays. They tended to be Chessentan-style swashbuckling tales popular in Faerûn, such as The Nymph Said No and The Moonlight Morningstar Murders. They were cheap, battered, and full of fanciful nonsense rather than useful lore, but even so the guests were warned that the guardian mage of the inn would magically track down any missing books and ensure their return.

History
The Niner was built around the mid-14 century DR when the wealthy adventurer Thurbrand made Arabel his base for excursions into the Stonelands, but couldn't find any lodgings to his tastes. He kept a suite at the inn for himself, in addition to at least one house elsewhere in the city.

## Ninthalor's harp

Ninthalor's harp was a magical harp used by the Harpers.

Powers
When played, Ninthalor's harp provided the player and their belongings with the effects of stoneskin, making even fragile items highly resistant to physical damage. Other creatures were not affected by this ability, even if they come into contact with the harpist.

Furthermore, an invisible, protective force field surrounded the harp and the player's arms, making it extremely difficult for others to physically interfere with the harpist's playing. However, this field did not protect against magical attacks and could be instantly ended by a silence spell with a radius of  or a hold person spell cast on the harpist.

History
Ninthalor's harp originated from the ancient city of Myth Drannor.

## Nik-Nuk

Tracker Nik-Nuk was a devoted novice in the Cult of the Absolute who served her goddess at the Shattered Sanctum during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Nik-Nuk had yellow-green skin and notably long ears. She wore her red-orange hair over to one side and decorated her face in white war paint.

Possessions
While she wore mostly simple clothes under a cracked leather vest, Nik-Nuk also protected herself with a few pieces of salvaged armor. In proud display, she wore an amulet bearing the holy symbol of the Absolute.

History
In the, Nik-Nuk and other members of the cult launched a devastating raid on the Waukeen's Rest inn. They slew many innocents, along with Flaming Fist soldiers, and took the captives that remained to the cult's fortress of Moonrise Towers.

After the raid, Nik-Nuk and two other cultists, Crub and Plagg, fantasized about how they would kill the adventurer Wyll, the self-proclaimed "Blade of Frontiers".

In order to better protect herself from such accidents, Nimreth weaved physical and spell protections into robes she cared enduring vestments. ## Niree Swifthands

Niree Swifthands was the high priestess of the Temple of the Half-Moon in Elmwood, as well as a werewolf servant of Selûne in the New Moon Pact in the 8 century DR.

Description
As a werewolf, Niree was a lean, light gray wolf.

Relationships
Niree was accompanied by her wolf friend Halftail, and was well-loved by the faithful of Elmwood.

History

Niree oversaw the Temple of the Half-Moon in Elmwood as high priestess, and served Selûne as a werewolf of the New Moon Pact, fighting against her enemies with tooth and claw. In 757 DR, the High Moonmistress Brima Chintamn of Bright Lady's Tower in Chancelgaunt declared the New Moon Pact a heresy, and Niree was among those targeted. She was captured, taken to Chancelgaunt, and executed by the church of Selûne at Bright Lady's Tower, though her companion Halftail escaped.

When Feena Archwood investigated the mystery of the New Moon Pact in 1373 DR, Niree's spirit appeared in her dreams alongside the other members of the Pact to defend and guide her. After she uncovered Velsinore's heresy and they mutually slew each other, Feena's spirit was met by a Shard escort to the afterlife, but the spirits of the Pact members encouraged her to return to life and join the New Moon Pact herself. With the aid of Julith Harkspur's healing, Feena was revived and ultimately continued the legacy of the Pact.

## Ninja-to

A ninja-to  was the standard weapon of a ninja.

Description
A ninja-to was a sword  in length, similar to a short sword, and was designed to be easily concealed. Unlike the katana, this sword featured a straight blade of somewhat lower quality. However, true to the ninja's tactical approach, both the sword and its scabbard possessed multiple functionalities, which varied among individual ninjas. For instance, some swords might contain concealed compartments in the hilt or tip of the scabbard, intended for carrying powders, poisons, or small daggers.

The scabbard, typically longer than the sword itself, was open at both ends, allowing it to double as a blowpipe or breathing tube. Additionally, a sturdy silk cord might be wrapped around the scabbard that was capable of being unraveled and utilized for climbing purposes. Due to its robust and rigid nature, the scabbard could even be used as a makeshift ladder rung.

Notable Ninja-to
 Ninjatō of the Scarlet Brotherhood, a ninja-to that originated from the world of Oerth.

## Niu Weidan

Niu Weidan was a thief of antiquity from the nation of Shou Lung in Kara-Tur who lived many generations before the 14 century DR.

History
Countless years before the, Niu Weidan made a name for himself as a notorious thief who wielded a powerful and deadly weapon – The Jade Fang. He used the dagger's magics to paralyze his opponents and then draining their life force to restore his own fleeting vitality. In the end, Niu Weidan's life was taken by the old age and not by an angry blade. Niu Weidan's descendants kept the The Jade Fang as a family heirloom for many generations, never using it in combat.

## Nickel

Nickel was a non-precious metal found in the Realms, used for a variety of purposes.

Properties
Pure nickel weighed  per . Prior to the Time of Troubles, nickel required a significant amount of progress on a psionicist's part before it could be synthesized through molecular rearrangement.

Availability
 The Elemental Store of Karsus enclave sold pure elemental nickel for 6 gp per .
 Deep-sea mineral nodules could contain concentrations of various metals, including nickel.
 Star metal was speculated to be made of nickel and iron.

Mining & Trade
Nickel was obtained from the ore pentlandite, a hard, brassy mineral that also contained iron.

 On the Sword Coast, Leilon mined especially rich deposits of nickel from the nearby Sword Mountains, as well as copper, iron, and silver. The metals were bought up cheaply by Waterdhavian merchants and then exported from the city.
 King's Reach was the smelting center of the Vast, where miners brought ore down from the mountains. The town exported nickel, copper, iron, silver, zinc, and gold.
 Cormyrean mines in the Storm Horns at Skull Crag provided nickel, as did the mines of Hillmarch in the Gnoll Peaks.
 In Amn, Eshpurta was a center of metalworking and forging, and was supplied with nickel, iron, electrum, and coal from mines in the Troll Mountains.
 In the High Forest, the Star Mounts had very pure nickel and iron deposits that were mined by the elves of Eaerlann, but since the fall of the realm they went untouched.
 Around the Lake of Steam following the Second Sundering, discoveries of nickel, iron, copper, and silver contributed to the growing wealth of the region.
 After shortages in Yikaria of nickel, iron, and copper, the Faceless God sent their followers to seek new deposits at Mount Hamarhaast in the Ice Spires.

Usages
 Other metals were alloyed with nickel, including silver. Halduthra was a peculiar alloy of nickel, tin, zinc, copper, and other minerals.
 Accessories and jewelry sometimes used nickel as a decorative element.
 The crossguards of swords were sometimes made of nickel, as were their pommels.
 A nickel tuning fork could be used to planeshift to the plane of Limbo.

Spell Components
 Binding used chains of nickel to affect creatures from Hades.
 Liquid earth used a valuable bent nickel tube.

Creatures
 Nalgs were immune to damage from mundane physical weapons, except from those made of nickel or a nickel alloy.

History
 The demon lord Kostchtchie wielded a huge cold iron hammer inlaid with nickel and silver.
 The archdevil Geryon carried a magical horn bound with brass bands and pins of nickel.

Other
 Planetoid shards (the destroyed fragments of planets in Wildspace) often had a sharp end made of nickel or iron.

## Nidoyama

Nidoyama, also known as Two-Peaks Mountain, was a mountain located in Kozakura.

Description
Nidoyama was located in western Maeshi Province on the island of Shinkoku. It was one of the few snow-covered peaks in Kozakura, boasting several small glaciers that graced its narrow cleft valleys.

Inhabitants
While Nidoyama seemed unremarkable at first glance, its significance lied in the enduring presence of korobokuru. Despite the majority of their kin having migrated to Tenmei ages ago, the korobokuru in this region steadfastly refused to emigrate and continued to call Nidoyama their home.

Additionally, the area surrounding Nidoyama was inhabited by numerous river spirit folk who held special ties to the run-off streams flowing from the glaciers.

## Nic'Epona

The nic'Epona, also known as Epona’s daughters, were a mystical breed of horses native to the Outlands that had the power to traverse the Outer Planes. Their latter name derived from rumors that they were direct descendants of the goddess Epona and that their powers thus derived from her.

Description
Physically a nic’Epona resembled a horse in all ways, with the exception that they had an innate ability to change the coloration of their hide into any found on a rainbow and beyond. Each individual typically adopted one color or color-pattern as their favorite, though could still change their hue based on their mood. Their bodies also had powerful teeth and quite sharp hooves. All nic’Epona were mares, though there were persistent rumors of males existing.

Personality
Though they resembled a mundane horse, nic'Epona were far smarter, possessing an exceptional level of intelligence. They were rather gregarious beings, finding enjoyment from the attention and company of others. Nic'Epona also delighted in toying with those that tried to magically charm them, pretending to fall under their sway and then abandoning them on an unfriendly or inhospitable plane, with Baator being a particular favorite spot to drop off irksome riders.

Abilities
The most striking innate ability of these creatures was the power to plane shift at will to anywhere in the Outer Planes, although the divine realms of some deities were inaccessible to them unless they were invited in. Activating this power required taking a couple of steps. When it activated a nic’Epona's hooves glowed with purple fire and a rainbow silhouette of their body persisted on the plane they left for a few moments.

Whenever and wherever nic’Epona galloped they could generate a momentary causeway beneath their hooves, which flared with a bright blue flame as they did so, that only they could cross for a period of one hour. This allowed them to walk over expanses of air, quicksand, and water as though they were flying. This power even allowed them to traverse up vertical surfaces.

Not only did it take weaponry with a +2 enchantment to harm a nic’Epona, but it was as though their whole bodies had a +2 enchantment over them, as they could harm any other creature requiring such a degree of magic to injure them. They were also unaffected by charm-related spells and any magic items whose effects included charming animals, though the latter immunity wasn't widely known.

Combat
Nic’Epona were fierce and fearless combatants. They fought their teeth and hooves just like any mundane horse, though the latter were said to hit with a severity comparable to a battleaxe. When with their herd, nic'Epona were likely to spread themselves out and mount a stampede against their aggressors. Whenever combat was not going in their favor, nic'Epona would often charge right at their opponent and plane shift away just moments before making contact with them.

Society
Nic'Epona were born from the union of a nic'Epona and another male equine, such as a mundane horse, a pegasus, or a unicorn.

These creatures were often found on their own out in the wider Outer Planes, but gathered in great herds on their home plane. These herds sometimes gathered around lochs on Prime Material worlds to frolic around and engage in races before determining a change in leadership. Herds were also fiercely protective of their young foals and all members would rise up to fight anyone that tried to kidnap one, chasing them all across the planes.
Homelands
Nic’Epona made their home in Tir na Og, a large divine realm in the Outlands belonging to the Celtic pantheon. They acted as defenders of the realm, riding in massive stampedes to overwhelm any who would threaten it.

Relationships
Nic’Epona could sometimes appear as manifestations of the Seldarine goddess Sehanine Moonbow. Beyond the service of deities, nic’Epona were regularly approached by various outsiders, planewalkers, petitioners, and individuals from the Prime Material with offers of favors in return for transport elsewhere in the Outer Planes. However, nic’Epona rarely did so unless their trust was first earned by treating them well, entertaining them, or even providing fine gifts. When treated particularly well they would allow a being to provide them a name to which they would respond to, coming to the individual's aid from elsewhere in the planes.

## Niskam Torlath

King Niskam Torlath "the Doomed" was an unfortunate Amnian monarch of the Torlath dynasty.

Relationships
Niskam was the great-grandson of King Imnel IV.

History
Niskam took the throne in the, following the assassination of King Imnel IV by the Skeletal Finger thieves' guild. Niskam failed to last the year, and was himself slain by agents of the Braethar merchant house who then installed Rhor Torlath as a puppet.

## Nine Sentinels

The Nine Sentinels, or Sentinel Towers, were a collection of prominent ancient towers scattered across the Tejarn Hills of southern Amn.

Description
The Sentinels remained mostly intact despite their great age, outlasting many other Calishite structures long fallen to ruin. They were built as simple outposts with small garrisons, with a signal-fire brazier and mirror atop used to communicate with their neighbors.

Geography
The Nine Sentinels were roughly evenly spaced across the length of the Tejarn Hills, from just above the Tethir Road near Brost in the west to the eastern edge beyond Hillfort Keshla.

History
The Sentinels were built during the Third Age of Calimshan ( to ), as part of a chain of army garrisons and signal towers scattered across the hills of what was then the wild frontier of the Ralamnish Ridings, linked by the South Road. The signal network was later joined with the city of Xandar in the Small Teeth, finished in 250 DR.

As the fortunes of Calimshan waxed and waned, the majority of these structures fell to ruin and disappeared into the rocky hills, but nine towers stubbornly remained over more than two-thousand years. Their original names were long lost, but gained new ones from an Amnian bard who titled each one in a song about the ghostly signals of the "Nine Sentinels."

## Night orchid

Night orchid was a species of the orchid family of flowering plants with deep dark blue and purple flowers, leaves, and stems.

Habitats
Night orchids were rare plants that could survive areas afflicted with shadow magic. One such location were the Shadow-cursed Lands around the Moonrise Towers of the Western Heartlands in the late 15 century DR.

Usages

Night orchids were valued for their alchemical properties. The plants could be used to extract a solution of night orchid out of them – a liquid solution of the color of evening. That solution, in turn, could be combined with certain types of alchemical vitriols to create superior elixirs of arcane cultivation.

Others simply valued night orchids for their beauty. One such individual was Shadowheart, a cleric of Shar from the late 15 century DR.

## Nine Wei clans

The Nine Wei clans were a collection of nine Shou clans who effectively controlled all of Shou political life on the Dragon Coast as of the late 15 century DR. "Wei" roughly translated to "power".

Activities
The Nine Wei included the clans who ruled over the Shou nation of Nathlan, but they also projected their power over the various Shou-towns throughout the Dragon Coast. Through their sponsorship of the Nine Golden Swords, they wielded further influence within the city of Westgate.

Individual members of the Nine Wei clans jockeyed against each other for power and influence in the Dragon Coast, and to curry favor with the Neng clan, the clan that led the Nine. The Neng clan was a "consort" clan of Shou Lung, and thus had strong ties to the Shou imperial court. Their leadership was said to be divine, and the clan set a highly lawful and traditionalist tone for the Nine Wei clans.

History
The Wei clans rose to power in the 15 century DR, although the roots of their influence stretched back centuries to the original arrival of the Neng clan in Nathlekh.

In the, a supposed heir to one of the Nine Wei clans conspired to destroy the Fan clan, another prominent Wei member. Under the pseudonym Madfox, he established a criminal syndicate known as the White Petal Tong in order to frustrate efforts to form a trade alliance between the Fan and the Neng clans. Mad Fox was ultimately revealed to be a member of the Tsui clan, a minor family from Nathlekh with aspirations of being counted among the Wei.

Members
Known clans among the Nine Wei included:
 The Fan clan, a Wei clan with close ties to the Nine Golden Swords as of the late 15 century DR.
 The Neng clan, the most influential of the Wei clans owing to their connections to imperial Shou Lung, and thus the leader among the Nine. The head of the clan was said to be a "walking god".

## Nickademus

Nickademus was one of the apprentices of the Mad Mage Halaster Blackcloak, who was responsible for the erection of a temple dedicated to Auril in Icewind Dale, made entirely out of ice in the early 14 century DR.

Description
Nickademus appeared to be a human man with dark skin and blue hair. He wore fanciful mage's robes. He was a reasonable well-spoken man.

History
In the, Nickademus was already a powerful wizard after leaving the tutelage of his master, the infamous Mad Mage of Undermountain. Some years before the Legion of the Chimera threatened the Ten-Towns, Nickademus fell in love with Orla, a priestess of Auril. The couple eventually drifted apart despite Nickademus still being in love. Orla's heart was poisoned by hate for Kuldahar and its heroes who slew a beloved priestess Lysan in the . This obsession and hate led Orla to fall under the sway of the genocidal Legion of the Chimera in 1212 DR, as it unleashed a force of monstrous humanoids and other inhabitants of the Frozenfar and the Spine of the World onto the Ten-Towns and Kuldahar.

Orla, the High Priestesses Sherincal, and their sisters became a significant part of the Legion's plan to dominate the north. She hired Nickademus, paying him hefty coin to create a massive temple complex of Auril. The wizard accepted the money, but he would've done it for his beloved for free. In just a single day, Nickademus poured all of his magic into the temple's erection, the feat that left him bedridden and unresponsive for some time. The clerics of Auril placed his sleeping body in one of the temple's bedrooms. In his design, Nickademus incorporated many "curiosities" throughout the temple, including mechanisms, traps, and magical devices. One of such magic curiosities was a huge painting Nickademus retrieved from Undermountain. This once-empty canvas was filled with imagery and turned into a magical transporter for the temple complex's inhabitants with a single command word. Another notable magic item he incorporated into the design was a large mirror of life trapping that later served to trap the mercenary band called The Talisman.

When Nickademus awoke from exhaustion, he met a group of mercenaries from Targos who opposed the Legion of the Chimera and were seeking passage through the temple. Nickademus lamented his beloved Orla's heart becoming consumed by darkness, and he understood what needed to be done to end the Aurilite threat. However, he asked for heroes' help in making sure that no other innocent would fall to hate and malice like Orla did. Nickademus offered advice to the adventurers, aided them in defeating the powerful spirit Aeij-Kllenzr't bound to the temple by the deity Talos, and shared several magic items with them, including a scroll of summon monster IV, a rogue stone, some iron rations, a magical returning frost dart, scrolls of protection from electricity, a ring of protection from electricity, and a delay poison potion.

Whether the heroes agreed to perform a favor for Nickademus in exchange for his help remained unknown. But some time later, the arch-mage would summon the adventurers back to the ice temple. His plan to make sure none other would ever fall to the darkness was simple: slay the fiends that were responsible for Orla's corruption. Nickademus summoned cornugons, gelugons, lemures, red and white abishai, and glabrezus, trapping them within the temple. He was unable to kill them by himself but would emerge victorious with the adventurers' help.

In the, Niram was among those who joined the new religion that sprang in the Western Heartlands. Niram abandoned his Lolthite faith in favor of the Absolute. He was stationed within the Moonrise Towers – the fortress controlled by the Absolute's warlord – General Ketheric Thorm. Zealot Niram stood guard of the Towers' throne room. Like many other members of the cult, Zealot Niram's fate remained unknown as he could've perished in battle when the High Harper Jaheira and her True Soul allies stormed the Moonrise.

## Nicodemus

Nicodemus was a sage and wizard who lived in the Stiltways of Yhaunn in the 14 century DR.

Description
The old sage had gray hair and a beard, with a bald spot developing atop his head. He had a habitual smile and a twinkle in his blue-gray eyes that made him look as if he was up to some sort of mischief.

Personality
Nicodemus was quite clever and thoughtful, though also very absent-minded and messy.

Abilities
As a wizard of some small power, Nicodemus knew a collection of handy spells for work and self-defense, including comprehend languages, detect magic, sleep, knock, and mirror image.

Possessions
His home and office was a cluttered mess of papers and trinkets that covered every surface.

In addition to his mundane scholarly supplies and resources, Nicodemus held a +1 ring of protection, a +1 dagger, a quill of everflowing ink, and a number of pills of water breathing.

Activities
Nicodemus lived in his shop in the Stiltways, providing services to the local population. He translated documents, bought and resold unusual books, wrote letters for the illiterate, and cast minor magics on demand. Although quite competent at his work, he was scatter-minded enough that he he was only ever just able to support himself.

History
In the 1360s DR, Nicodemus was approached by adventurers seeking his expertise regarding a navigator's rutter on behalf of Marla Wyverhold. During the negotiations over the item's worth, Nicodemus became quite flustered and accidentally knocked a candle onto the book, scorching it slightly. This revealed the invisible ink on the otherwise blank pages, revealing instructions that lead to the Oracle at Sumbar.

## Night of the Lunar Eclipse

Night of the Lunar Eclipse is a one round adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in June 1998 and is intended for adventurers of any level.

Synopsis

As a moon eclipse loomed over the city of Ravens Bluff, Armen Moonsilver of the Temple of Selûne assembled a group of heroes to find and return the fabled obsidian amulet of the Dark Maiden – the prison of Jessar, the demi-goddess of the dark side of the moon and the void, enemy of Selûne. The adventurers had a limited amount of time to find and retrieve the item before the goddess could break out. To make things even more complicated, the amulet could not be touched by a male as Jessar immediately destroyed a man's form and consumed his soul, while a female who touched it was immediately possessed. The heroes had to have a woman armed with a special silver gauntlet, contain the goddess within the items, and immediately return it to the Temple of Selûne to be destroyed. With the heroes' best efforts, the goddess either was freed, or her amulet swept away by the undercover followers of Jessar.

## Nightshield

Nightshield was a protective arcane and divine abjuration spell from the first circle of magic. The spell could be used by clerics, wizards, and sorcerers.

When the spell was cast, it enveloped the caster in a protective nighshield that boosted their resistance to harmful effects and increased their chances of avoiding harm or shrugging off harmful magics and effects. Additionally, the shield completely negated damage from all magic missiles. The spell's protective properties increased with the caster's magical proficiencies.

Components
The spell required only a somatic and verbal components.

## Nightmist

Nightmist was a Sharran Dark Justiciar Crusader in the hidden Cloister of Sombre Embrace in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Nightmist was a high elven woman with a light skin tone and blue eyes. Her features were hidden by a Dark Justiciar's helmet and a set of highly decorated plate mail. She was armed with a greatsword.

Abilities
Like other Sharran Justiciars, Nightmist was a formidable foe whose attacks were reinforced with necrotic magics. She could assume an umbral form when enveloped in darkness and launched devastating mental attacks, overwhelming her opponents with grief.

Relationships
Fellow Dark Justiciar Owltalon shared a close relationship of some sort with Nightmist, or "her little Nightmist," as the other woman referred to herself in their letter exchange. Nightmist eagerly reported to Owltalon that her mistress approved Nightmist's status as a Certified Venom Specialist and allowed her to study sudden esophageal collapse.

History
In the, during the Absolute crisis, Nightmist served the Mother Superior Viconia DeVir in the underground Cloister of Sombre Embrace as one of her cultists underneath the House of Grief. Nightmist, along with the entirety of the cloister, might have confronted a rogue member named Shadowheart, were she to turn away from Shar. Nightmist could have perished in the struggle. By other accounts, Nightmist and the rest of the cloister helped defend the city of Baldur's Gate from the army of the Absolute, were Shadowheart to remain loyal to her evil deity, or if Shadowheart was betrayed and surrendered to the Mother Superior for punishment.

## Nine-pin

Nine-pin was a game of skill played in a private room of the Growling Groghouse tavern in Undermountain.

Description
The gameplay of nine-pin involved rolling a polished ball down a  lane to hit a formation of nine pins. Each player took turns as the 'bowler' and had two attempts to knock over the pins. A 'strike' was declared if a player knocked over all nine pins in one bowl, and a 'spare' was when they knocked all nine down after their second bowl. If the lane still had pins remaining after two bowls, an 'open' was declared.

Strikes were worth 15 points, spares were worth 10 points, and a bowler who played an open received 1 point per knocked over pin.

Nine-pins required particular equipment to play. A bowler had to use a ball that was the right weight for them, and all players wore special shoes. The nine-pin alley in the Growling Groghouse was complete with a strong basket that caught balls and pins after they had been knocked over, and a bar that served drinks.

Using magic to influence the game was not necessarily against the rules.

Notable Nine-Pin Players
Halaster Blackcloak was particularly fond of nine-pin. He was a very competitive player, and took to insulting his opponents. He used the foresight spell to improve his chances at winning. When he beat someone in nine-pin, he forced them to serve him for a year and a day, transformed into a duplicate of himself. He put these duplicates to work in his nine-pin alley, cleaning and serving refreshments.

## Niall Carrathal

Nial Carrathal, born Niall Cymrych,  was a High King of Callidyrr in the mid–10 century DR. He was the first king of the Carrathal Dynasty, and although he held the title of High King of the Moonshae Isles, he only ruled over Callidyrr.

Relationships
He was the son of his predecessor, High King Tanner Cymrych, and he was succeeded by his first son, Erfle Carrathal.

History
Niall was born into the Cymrych Dynasty of High Kings, who traced their lineage to the legendary first High King, Cymrych Hugh. However, after the family lost the Crown of the High King with the death of Niall's great-great-grandfather, High King Dolan, and subsequently lost both control over Norland as well as possession of the Sword of Cymrych Hugh with the death of his father, High King Tanner, the family's legitimacy as well as its connection to its legendary ancestor had been badly weakened. Thus, when Niall assumed the throne in the, the rule of the true High Kings of the Moonshae Isles came to an end, and he became High King of Callidyrr only. Niall made the decision to change the family name to Carrathal at this time, a move which was imitated when several other noble families likewise changed their names to distance themselves from the surname "Cymrych".

Niall ruled for ten years before dying of illness in the .

## Night Brigade

The Night Brigade was a militant gang of Sharran Wheloon Prisoners led by the devoted priestess of Shar Storna Harnwood in the late 15 century DR. They enjoyed a period of growing power and influence inside the prison until they were finally destroyed by the forces of the rival Riverside Rats gang.

Organization

The gang's various interests were overseen by different officers within the organization. They acted semi-independently, and each received a share of the gang's profits that they used for their own desires and goals.

 Storna Harnwood oversaw the temple headquarters itself, saw to the spiritual health of her congregation, and directed the overall operations of the gang. She kept no treasure for her own pleasure, but spent it all on glorifying Shar and spreading the worship.
 Battlemaster Agarta, though bloodthirsty and impetuous, handled the professional training of the gang's fighters. She also managed a set of arenas where willing and unwilling gladiators fought to the death for entertainment, bringing in money for the gang.
 Master Thief Karissa led her own "guild" of thieves and assassins, and was responsible for stealing huge amounts of loot within the prison, as well as digging her own secret tunnel network beneath the city. She laired in her own opulent hideout and disdained outside interactions.
 Grand Arcanist Darstil Vurusk performed insane experiments in his mage tower and was rather unhappy to actually take part in gang activities. Regardless, he still helped the gang by providing training for lesser wizards, created twisted monsters that spied on the prison, and supplied whatever arcane expertise Storna needed.

Base of Operations
The Night Brigade's ultimate home was the Temple of Shar they built atop the ruins of the Riverside Rats hideouts. The impressive temple was well-appointed and well-defended, and served as a true stronghold of their power in the city.

History

The gang was founded by the devotee of Shar, Storna Harnwood, and it was by her strong will that the gang was able to slowly win wealth, power, and influence over the prison. Their finest moment was the near-destruction of the Riverside Rats gang, which let them build their Temple of Shar on the ruins of their old rats nest. Unfortunately for them, this led to the Rats seeking allies outside of the prison, who proceeded to lead a retributive strike on the Night Brigade with the assistance of talismans that allowed shapeshifting into rats. First Battlemaster Agarta was slain in her own arena, then Master Thief Karissa was cornered in her own hideout, and Grand Arcanist Darstil was killed while obsessing over his experiments. Finally, with the blessings of Selûne helping to throw back Shar's darkness, they attacked the Temple of Shar and slew Storna Harnwood, utterly breaking the gang and ending its reign over the city.

## Nildon Baraejhe

Nildon Baraejhe was a Zhentarim and poisoner in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
He could crack a joke or two.

Activities
He traveled all across Faerûn, bringing his poisons with him.

Relationships
He was a member of the Zhentarim who worked with Calivar Murpeth.

History
In the, Nildon Baraejhe was at a revel in Warmfires House, Shadowdale, where Calivar Murpeth, a Zhentarim, tried to poison the scrivener Maervidal Iloster. While their guest drank wine with saisha poison in it, Nildon explained that the additive was made of several different herbs. The scrivener immediately ran off for the jakes, with Baraejhe moving too slowly to block him. Later, he joined Calivar Murpeth upstairs, as they began the interrogation of Talantha, apparently a local sex worker who may have aided Maervidal in escaping. He took the disappearance of the scribe in stride.

He later took part in the torture of Talantha himself, giving her a few slaps first. After she revealed herself to be Storm Silverhand and Harpers burst into the room, all of the Zhentarim in the room were captured, including Nildon. (They hadn't really poisoned Maervidal Iloster either; unknown to them, he was actually Storm Silverhand in disguise too.)

## Nightmare, the Fallen Moon

Nightmare, the Fallen Moon was a powerful elven moonblade bastard sword uncovered in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Nightmare was a gruesomely deadly blade, even beyond its supernatural keenness. It held entropic energy that unmade reality, vampirically drained the vitality from victims, and mentally attacked them with waking nightmares in the manner of mind thrust and phantasmal killer. If that wasn't enough, the sword's powers could be further augmented in combination with a planar focus of erudition, planar focus of prowess, or planar focus of subterfuge recovered from Lolth's realm.

The blade possessed a malevolent will of its own, and Nightmare willingly served any who were strong enough to claim it, if only for a time.

History
Nightmare was once a traditional elven moonblade, passed down in the family to be wielded by the virtuous. The blade was somehow lost and corrupted with fell magic, becoming a thing of malice and destruction. During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Elminster directed heroes from Eberron to invade Lolth's realm and foil her plans with Ana Brabener and the Demon Weave, protecting their home and Faerûn in the process. Nightmare may have ended up in their hands at the end of the adventure.

## Ningtang Road

The Ningtang Road was a road in Ch'ing Tung Province in Shou Lung. It ran from the city of Shou'Kuan westward to the city of Yenching in Ma' Yuan Province.While mentioned in text, the Ningtang Road is not depicted on any map. The Forgotten Realms Atlas appears to extend the Kuan Postroad westward, but it deviates away from Yenching.

History
In late  or early, increasing bandit activity along the Ningtang Road east of Yenching made travel dangerous and expensive. Thus, a local merchants guild hired the chanshi Zarwu Koi to protect their caravans. Exploring the western hills of Ching Tung and posing as a bandit himself, Zarwu found and joined the group, learning they were from Chukei Province and were journeying south to prey on travelers and tourists visiting the Komite in Suijeng. He killed them as they slept and took their heads back to the merchants guild.

## Nil

Nil was an enigmatic spy who operated in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Nil had pale skin and shoulder-length auburn hair. She carried a lute, a shortsword, and wore a short, low-cut white dress and sandals.

Personality
Nil was an expert manipulator and master of subterfuge who refused to be relegated or bound to any particular group or cause, and was drawn to drama, performance, and grandiosity. In spite of this, she was broadly sympathetic to Cormyr and the Harpers, and was opposed to the forces of Thultanthar.

History

Nil was approached by the Harpers to be recruited as an agent, but she refused, preferring to chart her own whimsical path. At some point she ended up infiltrating Wheloon Prison during the Shadovar invasion during the mid-1480s DR, and appeared to agents of the Purple Dragons when they delved into the depths of the prison, mysteriously sitting in the bowl of a large brazier while playing her instrument. Unable to proceed on her own mission to steal documents from Shar Manor, she provided the key to a locked room in the Manor and gave directions to the agents. Naturally, she refused to explain why she wanted the documents.

After her adventure in Wheloon, she joined forces with the fearsome mercenary Dom when they were hired by Returned Netheril to steal a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll, cultivating a persona as an amoral hireling. She fed information about the elite mercenaries to the Harpers without revealing her own identity, spilling details about Dom, Thoven, Udere, Rand, Pelloth, Song, Dolthin, and herself as they prepared ambushes along Greenweald Lane. When they finally met Bensen Virandson in battle, Nil led a team of mercenaries and grandly presented herself as an arch-nemesis, narrating in the third-person, before suddenly surrendering and revealing her status as a double agent. Fortunately for her, she survived this plot twist and lived to be dramatic some other day.

## Niri Alud

Niri Alud was a conjurer from Tethyr who had a passion for chess and created his own magical chess pieces like the Black Knight.

History
Niri was known to travel with chess sets on his voyages. Because of the precarious nature of the Realms, he wanted the ability to summon allies at a moment's notice without giving away his intentions. He created a wide variety of chess sets of different colors that would summon constructed, golem-like automatons to aid him in battle.

When he was attacked by desert raiders in Calimshan, almost all of the wizard's chess sets were lost. The raiders, ignorant of arcane ways, let most of the chess sets sink into the sands. The chess sets that were taken were soon separated and the pieces traveled through trade and theft. Treasure hunters continued to search for the missing pieces of those sets.
